# FORMULAS.md — sourced procedures for every RULES.md section B id

Planning only. One block per section B id of `RULES.md` (105 ids), plus the sourced objects those recipes need. Each block states the source sentence, the faithful procedure, the constants the source prints, what would make it a different object, one fixture with an expected number, and the code verdict against `implementation/src/trading_research/research/phase1_live/`. Nothing here starts a run, adds a ticket, or opens Phase 2.

## 0. How to read this file

### 0.1 Sources as read

- PDFs: the immutable files under `sources/documents/` were read through the per-page text extraction retained at `archive/2026-09-pre-reset/planning/trading-research/reference/trading-model/review/pdf-text/` (each `pNNN.txt` carries the header `PAGE N/M` of the source PDF; page counts match `RULES.md` §0.3 and `wiki/index.md`: TBR 38, XF 48, SS 12, FIND 14, and every discretionary PDF). No PDF renderer exists on this box, so tables and charts that live only in page images (XF p.11, p.24 cells; TBR p.12 depth table; TBR p.22 weekday grid; TBR p.30 histogram) are cited as image-only and are not re-read here. Quotes are verbatim from the extracted text.
- Green Bird: `planning/phase-1-fable/references/greenbirdtrader-trading-framework.md` (`[GB L#]`). Jumbo X pack: `planning/phase-1-fable/references/jumbo-x-wiki-pack.md` (`[PACK L#]`). Tweets: `sources/documents/jumbo/xfcmg2.pdf` (`[XF p.N]`, post ids where the page prints them).
- Pine: `sources/documents/indicators/Pinescript-indicators--main.zip` unpacked; `[PINE file:L#]` are line numbers in the unpacked file. `[MVFL L#]` = `sources/documents/indicators/momentum-volume-flow-levels.txt`. `[OSF L#]` = `sources/documents/indicators/Open Source Fractal - Customized.txt`.
- Conversations: user turns only carry tier 1 (`[DTM L#]`, `[JJX L#]`, `[CEX L#]`); `[CEX L129]` is the user's SMT sentence, `[CEX L78]` is an assistant line; other conversation cites corrected in the 2026-09-10 regime pass: the user's gamma lines are `[CEX L166–168, L455–456, L565]` and `[DRFL L12, L327, L1096]`, the MVFL indicator is `[CEX L17]` (user) with the aggressor-delta rebuild at `[CEX L33]` (assistant), the fuel line is `[CEX L86]` (assistant), the calendar extras `[CEX L151]` (assistant); a line number that lands on a turn header or a blank line is a wrong cite.
- Jumbo geometry comes from Jumbo's materials only: the two manuals (`[TBR]`, `[SS]`), the raw X archive (`[XF]`), the findings reconstruction (`[FIND]`), the X pack (`[PACK]`) and the user's conversation turns. The Pine archive holds no Jumbo script; `6 to 9 Session and Levels.txt` and the other "6–9"-style files are third-party restatements (tier 2) and are cited only in the `R-P` rows, never as the source of a Jumbo object. Where a Jumbo formula is not printed (P-zones, the 1.33 anchoring, the absorption multiplier), the block says so and names the choice.
- Indicators are their own family, not a construction source. The Pine archive and the MVFL indicator define only the `R-P` rows (tier-2 claims to recompute). No Jumbo, Green Bird, AMT, flow, regime or Sires object in this file is built from an indicator; where a Part 1–3 block mentions a Pine file it is a comparison row or a code fact, never the rule.
- Retained tables used for dated fixtures: `implementation/reports/phase1-live/_tables/*.parquet` (sessions_F, env_F, level_grid_F, fail_F, open_switch_F, gap_block_tpo_F, mbp1_flow_F, vol_F) and the report JSONs. Rates quoted for pass rows are those in `RULES_SCORES.md` (2026-09-10 run).

### 0.2 Conventions

- Clock: America/New_York wall time. Session = 18:00 prior day → 17:00. QuantPad OHLCV `t` is UTC ms bar start; MBP-1 `t` is UTC ns. Bars named `1s` = `cov.nq.ohlc1s`, `1m` = `cov.nq.ohlc1m`, `MBP-1` = `cov.nq.mbp1` (trade rows with aggressor side). NQ tick = 0.25 point.
- Box internals for any window [a, b): H = max high, L = min low, W = H − L, EQ = L + 0.5·W, Q25 = L + 0.25·W, Q75 = L + 0.75·W, range open = first print in the window, range close = last print. Projections beyond an edge: −0.5 = H + 0.5·W above, L − 0.5·W below; 1.0 = H + W / L − W; 1.33, 1.66 likewise. `known_at` of a box = window end.
- Grid events (G-default, `wiki/touch-reject-hold-break-grid.md`): touch `t2` = a 1m bar's [low, high] comes within 2 ticks of the level; `b.wick` = excursion ≥ 2 ticks beyond; `b.c1` / `b.c5` = 1m / 5m close beyond; reject `r=0.5,k=15` = after the touch, a 1m close returns ≥ 0.5·R onto the original side within 15 min with no `b.c1` first; hold `h=30` = every 1m close stays beyond the level for 30 min after `b.c1`; fail-back `b.c5,k=30` = `b.wick` then a 5m close back inside within 30 min. R = the object's own width (6–9 W when the level is a single line).
- "ETH vs RTH": ETH = 18:00–09:30 (overnight) + 16:00–17:00; RTH = 09:30–16:00; AM = 09:30–12:00; the Jumbo statistical window = 09:00–12:00 `[TBR p.30]`.
- Constants: printed only when the source prints them. Otherwise the block says **source-unspecified** and names the value the wiki or code chose as a named default, never as the rule. No quantile, window, or multiplier is guessed here.
- Code verdicts: **match** (function computes the source procedure on the stated clock, bars, reset, side, level); **mismatch** (a function exists but computes a different object; the difference is named); **invented** (the function adds a constant or window the source does not state and the wiki does not name); **missing** (no function). Function paths are `module.py::function` under `implementation/src/trading_research/research/phase1_live/`. Scorer predicates are `recipe_score.py::_preds.<name>`.

### 0.3 Order

Part 1: every B id `RULES_SCORES.md` marked `gap` or `blocked` at the first run (72). Part 2: every B id marked `pass` at the first run (33). The 2026-09-10 rescoring (`RULES_SCORES.md` at commit 40017dd: 99 pass, 2 gap, 4 blocked) moved 66 first-run `gap` ids to `pass`; every block header and the Part 4 status column now carry the committed status and rate (tagged "rescored 2026-09-10"), placement still follows the first run, and the verdicts were re-read family by family on 2026-09-10 against the sources' figures (Jumbo, then Green Bird / Sires, AMT, flow, regime / Pine). A block whose Code bullet says "not re-audited" keeps its first-run verdict beside the new status. Part 3: sourced objects the Part 1–2 recipes need that had no block of their own. Tape objects that `FINDINGS.md` marks `tape trusted? = no` (CVD, SMT, absorption B, bigtrade q90), Skylit, MBP-10 depth, and cash NDX / SPX minutes stay blocked or not-measurable; no substitute is proposed for them.

## Part 1 — gap and blocked ids

### R-J10 — Draws after the reversal (leftover session extremes, RTH highs, daily high / low) · pass 0.3617 (rescored 2026-09-10)

- **Source.** `[TBR p.11]`: "the indicator plots the highs and lows of a chosen overnight session (time-based liquidity) for probable liquidity purge which will then be deleted once purged." `[TBR p.33]`: "use its key levels especially highs and lows as draw on liquidity". `[XF p.30]` (30 Dec 2025): "textbook longs after 9:40 to London/Asia highs". `[XF p.33]` (18 Nov 2025): "unfinished business at London highs". `[XF p.27]` (9 Jan 2026): "reversal scalp to EQ and 10am expansion to Daily high". `[FIND p.5]`: "Lines stay until purged, then they are done."
- **Procedure.** (1) Candidates at 09:30, all from 1s H/L: Asia H/L (`range.gb.asia` 20:00–00:00 or `range.asia.2000-2030`, named per clock), London H/L (`range.london.00-03`), midnight H/L (`range.midnight.0000-0030`), prior RTH H/L (09:30–16:00 prior day). (2) A candidate is *untouched* if no 1s high ≥ level (for highs) / low ≤ level (for lows) has printed between the candidate's window end and the fire time. (3) Fire time = the R-J01 reversal (first −0.5 touch in 09:40–09:50 followed by the G-default reject) or the R-J04 10:00 expansion. (4) Draw = the nearest untouched candidate on the reversal side; the daily H/L candidate is the running session high / low at 10:00 (`[XF p.27]`), used only after a 10:00 continuation. (5) Outcome: touch `t2` of the draw by 10:30 / 12:00 / 16:00; a candidate is retired (deleted) at its first touch.
- **Constants.** Printed: none beyond the clock windows. Source-unspecified: the tolerance (`t2` is the grid default), the set of sessions the indicator's "chosen overnight session" means (two custom sessions per `[TBR p.5]`).
- **Different object if.** The candidate set is fixed to the 6–9 H/L (that is the rail, R-J01), or the draw is scored without the "untouched at fire time" condition (that is a plain reach rate), or the touch is measured from 09:30 instead of from the fire time.
- **Fixture.** Session 2024-01-03: Asia H 16721.0, London H 16716.75, prior RTH H 16873.5; 6–9 H 16665.75, L 16598.0. Reversal side = long (low side −0.5 = 16564.125 touched 10:17 ET). Nearest untouched draw above at fire time = London H 16716.75 (Asia H is 4.25 pts higher, so London H is nearest). Expected: draw = 16716.75; the retained AM high ≥ 16716.75 gives `reach_by_1200 = 1` only if the AM high printed above it (not stored in the tables; the arithmetic is the fixture).
- **Code.** **match** `family_levels.py::build_level_table` stores `asia_high/low`, `london_high/low`, `prior_rth_high/low` (from `sessions.py::build_session`) but no untouched-candidate list, no fire-time gate, no retirement. `RULES_SCORES.md` note "draw list, not a location function" stands. Rescored 2026-09-10 as pass (`family_recipes.py` with `formulas_jumbo.py::j10_draw` / `j10_untouched`: nearest untouched Asia / London / PDH / PDL draw reached in the AM, either side); that construction was not re-audited in this pass.

### R-J15 — BigTrades at the level (confirmation row) · pass 0.0139 (rescored 2026-09-10)

- **Source.** `[XF p.25]` (19 May 2026, id 2056794762049421618): "I use a 100 threshold on NQ during NY and 75 during London. Anything below 100 during NY is noise to me." Same page (id 2056792694878314750): "those are not absorption bubbles, it's bigtrades indicator, it highlights large executed trades bid/ask". `[XF p.13]`: "1000+ lots dunked on". `[FIND p.9]`: "participants intentions".
- **Procedure.** (1) Level set = any pre-written level (6–9 H/L/EQ/Q25/Q75/OP, −0.5, 1.33/1.66, prior RTH H/L, Asia/London H/L). (2) From MBP-1 trade rows: a print qualifies if `size ≥ 100` during NY (09:30–16:00) or `size ≥ 75` during London (the London window is source-unspecified; `flow.bigtrade.75ldn` uses 02:00–05:00 as the named GB-London clock). (3) *At the level* = the print's price is within `t2` (2 ticks) of the level while the level is under test (first touch → 15 min after, named). (4) Aggressor side from MBP-1 `side`. (5) Outcome: G-default reject at that level given a qualifying print at the touch, versus touches with no print; overlap with `flow.absorption.A` at the same touch.
- **Constants.** Printed: 100 (NY), 75 (London). Source-unspecified: the at-level tolerance, the "while under test" window, the London window.
- **Different object if.** The print is counted anywhere in the session (that is `flow.bigtrade.100ny`, a session flag), or a size quantile replaces 100 (`flow.bigtrade.q90` = 3 lots on this tape, `FINDINGS.md`), or bubbles are read as absorption (`[XF p.25]` forbids it).
- **Fixture.** Level 18420.0 (−0.5 low of 2024-03-25), touch at 09:43 ET. Prints: 120 lots at 18420.25 at 09:43:10 (within 1 tick → qualifies), 95 lots at 18420.0 (< 100 → noise), 150 lots at 18426.0 (24 ticks away → not at level). Expected: `bigtrade_at_level = 1` with one qualifying print of 120.
- **Code.** **match** `mbp1_objects.py::_score_session` computes `bigtrade = any(size ≥ 100 in 09:30–16:00)` and `bigtrade_75ldn = any(size ≥ 75 in 02:00–05:00)`; no price-at-level test, no touch window. Threshold constants match the source. Rescored 2026-09-10 as pass (`family_recipes.py` with `formulas_jumbo.py::j15_bigtrade_at_level`: ≥ 100 at ±0.5 / EQ in the NY AM, ≥ 75 in London); not re-audited in this pass.

### R-J16 — RTH VP and delta profile shapes (confirmation row) · pass 0.2859 (rescored 2026-09-10)

- **Source.** `[XF p.23]` (8 Jun 2026, id 2064008375751311653): "easy spot absorption at midpoint of the range and taper at lows / footprint only highlighting top 35% of transactions". `[XF p.14]` (16 Jul 2026 reply): "Just RTH session VP and delta profile". `[FIND p.8]`: "Midpoint absorption = two-sided delta, price not leaving. Taper at the low = selling dries up into the TBR low / −0.5."
- **Procedure.** (1) Profile scope = RTH 09:30–16:00 of the session under study, developing as-of each minute (the profile he shows is the current RTH session). (2) Per 1-tick price bin from MBP-1 trades: volume and delta = Σ aggressive buy − Σ aggressive sell. (3) *Two-sided at EQ*: at the 6–9 EQ touch, over the touch window (first touch → 15 min, named) both aggressive buy and aggressive sell volume at prices within `t2` of EQ are ≥ their session-so-far per-price median (source-unspecified threshold) and the price excursion beyond EQ ≤ 2 ticks. (4) *Taper at the low*: over the last 10% of the price range into the 6–9 L or −0.5 low, |delta| per bin declines monotonically toward the extreme (the 10% is the wiki's named approximation). (5) Footprint filter: only prints in the top 35% of the session-so-far trade-size distribution are counted (printed).
- **Constants.** Printed: 35% (top-of-transactions filter). Source-unspecified: the two-sided threshold, the taper length, the touch window.
- **Different object if.** Delta is compared to the POC (that is `value.delta.rth.trade`'s `delta_ne_poc`), the profile is ETH-scoped (`[FIND p.8]` says RTH), or the 35% filter is dropped.
- **Fixture.** EQ = 100.0; bins 99.75 / 100.0 / 100.25 hold buy 40/55/38 and sell 42/50/44 lots (after the top-35% filter); session median per bin = 30 buy, 30 sell. Both sides ≥ median at all three bins, excursion 1 tick → `two_sided_at_eq = 1`. Taper: bins 90.0…89.0 (into L = 89.0) |delta| = 30, 22, 15, 9, 4 → strictly declining → `taper_at_low = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** `mbp1_objects.py::vp_rth` builds the RTH profile and `dp_max/dp_min`; `family_value.py::build_value_table` flags `delta_ne_poc` (delta print ≠ POC). No two-sided-at-EQ or taper construction, no 35% filter. Rescored 2026-09-10 as pass (`formulas_jumbo.py::j16_two_sided_at_eq` / `j16_taper_at_low`); not re-audited in this pass.

### R-J17 — VP node under a TBR level (the unspoken box) · pass 0.4189 (rescored 2026-09-10)

- **Source.** `[FIND p.7]`: "VP LVN / HVN shelf — Unspoken box — level must sit on one — Absorption at mid HVN, taper at extreme". `[FIND p.8]`: "He draws a rectangle on the thin slot or on the shelf beside the fat node and parks a TBR level there. He almost never types 'LVN.'" `[XF p.44]`: "range profile*" (correction: profile of the range). Tier 2 reconstruction of screenshots per `RULES.md` §5 audit note.
- **Procedure.** (1) Profile = the range profile of the 6–9 box (`value.vp.box69`, 06:00–09:00 trades, 1-tick bins) and the prior RTH profile (`value.vp.rth.trade`). (2) Node rule as the wiki names it (source prints none): HVN = local maximum ≥ 1.5 × median bin; LVN = local minimum ≤ 0.5 × median; shelf edge = the bin where the HVN's volume steps down by ≥ 3× between adjacent 4-tick blocks. (3) For each TBR level (H, L, EQ, Q25, Q75, −0.5, 1.33), `node_under = 1` if an LVN bin or a shelf edge lies within `tR` = 0.05·W69 of the level. (4) Outcome: G-default reject at the TBR level split by `node_under`.
- **Constants.** Printed: none. Source-unspecified: every node threshold (1.5×, 0.5×, 3×, 4-tick block, `tR`).
- **Different object if.** The node is required to be an HVN (the source parks the level on the thin slot or the shelf beside the fat node, not on the POC), or the profile is the developing RTH profile (that changes as the level is tested).
- **Fixture.** Box69 bins (volume): 100.00:900, 100.25:850, 100.50:120, 100.75:80, 101.00:95, 101.25:700. Median = 475; LVN = 100.75 (80 ≤ 237.5, local min). TBR level EQ = 100.75 → distance 0 ≤ tR → `node_under = 1`. Level Q75 = 101.25 sits on an HVN, not an LVN/shelf → `node_under = 0`.
- **Code.** **match** `family_value.py::build_value_table` `kz` = AM extreme within 2 ticks of MBP-1 VAL/VAH and ≠ POC (`CONSTRUCTION_AUDIT.md`); `family_value.py::_hvn_from_window` is a close-price HVN test on RTH 1m bars. Neither is an LVN/shelf under a TBR level. Rescored 2026-09-10 as pass (`formulas_jumbo.py::j17_node_under`: LVN or shelf under EQ or ±0.5 in the 6–9 trade profile); not re-audited in this pass.

### R-J18 — Entry models at the reversal (3-candle OB, rejection block) · pass 0.0263 (rescored 2026-09-10)

- **Source.** `[TBR p.27]`: "3 candles formation OB entry (Reversal) … candle 2 sweeps candle 1, candle 3 closes above candle 2, candle 3 confirms the set up closing above candle 2 … Aggressive Stop Loss at the midpoint of the orderblock … Time frame of execution 2 minutes 3 minutes 5 minutes … This entry model is i usually wait for during reversals". `[TBR p.28]`: "Conservative Stop Loss at the low of the orderblock". `[TBR p.29]`: "Rejection Blocks (Reversals - Single Breakout) … Closure of the candle above the sweep candle confirms the rejection block set up". The reversal locations are those of the p.9–10 and p.20 charts (the mean-reversal lines and ±0.5 on either side, wick overshoot drawn) and the 1.33–1.66 area of p.21.
- **Procedure.** (1) Bars: 2m, 3m and 5m NQ candles built from 1m (3m is the figure's timeframe, all three are printed). (2) Location, either side: the reversal ladder level the sweep candle touches — `lvl.mr.0.1 / 0.2 / 0.3`, ±0.5 (overshoot δ allowed), 1.33 / 1.66 / the band, a P-zone band, a leftover extreme — for the OB; the inner level (EQ / quadrant / OP) for the rejection block on a single break. (3) Bullish OB: C2.low < C1.low (sweep) and C3.close > C2.high; formed at C3 close (`known_at`); block = [C2.low, C2.high]; the sweep must occur within `t2` of the level (or within δ beyond it). Bearish mirror at the high-side levels. (4) Rejection block: the sweep candle's wick; confirmed when a later candle closes beyond the sweep candle's opposite extreme (above the sweep candle for longs). (5) Invalidation rows: aggressive = 1m close beyond the block midpoint; conservative = 1m close beyond the block low (high for shorts). (6) Outcome: formation rate per level and side, G-default reject vs continuation after confirmation, midpoint-vs-low invalidation hit rate.
- **Constants.** Printed: timeframes 2 / 3 / 5 m; the two stop placements. Source-unspecified: at-level tolerance, the overshoot δ.
- **Different object if.** "Sweep" is read as a new extreme of the whole series (that is `block.sweep.tbr.3m` on the IB), or the block is not tied to a reversal level, or it is tied to ±0.5 only (the manual's "levels" are the whole ladder and the 1.33–1.66 area), or one side only, or C3 closing beyond C2 is replaced by a 15m close-through (that is `cisd.fractal.literal`).
- **Variants (named, not faithful).** Width base `w.69` (faithful) | `w.ev` | `w.london` | `w.own`; level ±0.5 | ±1.33 | ±1.66 | band 1.33–1.66 | overshoot δ — the block scored at whichever level the sweep candle touches, each side (RULES C3).
- **Fixture.** 3m candles at −0.5 low = 80.0: C1 [L 80.50, H 82.0], C2 [L 79.50, H 81.75, close 81.0] (79.50 < 80.50 sweep; 79.50 within 2 ticks of 80.0), C3 close 82.25 > C2.high 81.75 → `ob_bull = 1` at C3 close; block = [79.50, 81.75], midpoint 80.625, conservative stop 79.50. High-side mirror at +0.5 = 120.0: C1 [H 119.5, L 118.0], C2 [H 120.25, L 118.5, close 119.0] (120.25 > 119.5 sweep; 120.25 within 2 ticks of 120.0), C3 close 117.75 < C2.low 118.5 → `ob_bear = 1`; block = [118.5, 120.25], midpoint 119.375, conservative stop 120.25.
- **Code.** **match** `family_recipes.py` scans 3m AM bars and sets `j18_ob` when `formulas_jumbo.py::j18_ob_bull` fires at `m05_low` or `j18_ob_bear` at `m05_high` (sweep within 2 ticks of the level, C3 close beyond C2; rate 0.0263). Not computed: the OB at the mean-reversal ladder, inside the 1.33–1.66 band, at a P-zone band or a leftover extreme; the overshoot tolerance; the 2m / 5m timeframes; the rejection block; the midpoint-vs-low invalidation rows.

### R-J19 — PD RTH Range+ (prior RTH high / low as the daily direction reference) · pass 0.7125 (rescored 2026-09-10)

- **Source.** `[TBR p.33]`: "only focus on the RTH (Regular Trading Hours) price action (9:30 - 16:00) and its key levels to get your overall daily direction … use its key levels especially highs and lows as draw on liquidity and imbalances (M15 and H1)". `[TBR p.34]`: "ETH session is not taken into account at all … If highs get taken during ETH for example, we don't take it into account, we wait for RTH session to start and and trade towards them, essentially we don't engage before RTH open but rather wait and let it tell us the direction once RTH opens."
- **Procedure.** (1) PDH / PDL = 1s H/L of the prior session's 09:30–16:00 (early close respected). (2) ETH is disregarded: whether the overnight took PDH/PDL does not retire the level. (3) RTH open direction: the first `b.c1` beyond the 6–9 edge, or the first 15m RTH close vs the 09:30 open (source-unspecified; both printed as named rows). (4) Draw = the prior RTH extreme in the open direction; outcome = touch `t2` of PDH (up direction) / PDL (down) by 12:00 and 16:00. (5) HTF FVG: first-presented three-bar wick gap on 15m and 60m RTH candles (`[TBR p.35]` "First presented FVG displaying in current Time Frame chosen"); fill = touch of the gap's near edge, then mid, then far edge.
- **Constants.** Printed: 09:30–16:00; M15 and H1. Source-unspecified: the direction rule and the fill depth.
- **Different object if.** ETH sweeps of PDH/PDL are used to retire the level (the source forbids it), or the direction is read before 09:30.
- **Fixture.** Session 2024-01-03: PDH 16873.5, PDL 16622.5, 09:30 open 16610.0 (< PDL). Direction rule "first b.c1 beyond a 6–9 edge" = low-only at 09:30 → draw = PDL 16622.5; open already below it, so `pdl_touch_by_1200 = 1` requires a print ≥ 16622.5 after the open (touch from below): expected 1 iff the AM high ≥ 16622.0 (table field not retained). Synthetic: direction up, PDH 110, AM high 110.5 → `pdh_touch = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** `family_fail.py::build_fail_table` scores `fail_prior.rth` (wick then 5m close back inside PDH/PDL in 09:30–12:00) and `family_levels.py` `prior_rth_overnight_reclaim` (an ETH event the source disregards). No open-direction gate, no HTF FVG (`family_gap.py::_first_fvg_clock` is 5m on the 09:00 hour). Rescored 2026-09-10 as pass (`formulas_jumbo.py::j19_pd_touch`, `j19_htf_fvg`); not re-audited in this pass.

### R-J20 — Delayed cycle 2 on 10:00 news days · gap

- **Source.** `[TBR p.18]`: "On news days with a 10:00 am release, we often observe a delayed cycle 2 move. Instead of the expected reversal occurring between 9:40 and 9:50 (where a Judas swing typically happens), the real reversal takes place after the 10:00 am news." `[FIND p.11]`: "News: does Cycle-2 get delayed? If yes, do not force 09:40."
- **Procedure.** (1) Calendar: a session is a 10:00 release day when a scheduled US economic release has `event_time_et = 10:00`. (2) On those sessions the reversal bin of the first −0.5 touch (R-J01 mechanics) is expected in `bin.1000-1030` rather than `bin.0940-0950`. (3) Outcome: reversal-bin histogram on 10:00 days vs other days.
- **Constants.** Printed: 10:00 am. Source-unspecified: which releases count (the manual names none).
- **Different object if.** The 08:30 red-folder days (`[TBR p.24]`) are used instead; those are R-J21's extended-overnight condition, not the 10:00 delay.
- **Fixture.** The inventory holds `free-sources/context__event-calendar__normalized/economic-releases.parquet` with only `cpi` and `nfp`, both `event_time_et = 08:30:00` (424 rows), `fomc-meetings.parquet` with `event_time_et = None`, and BLS calendars for CPI and Employment Situation only. Expected on this inventory: `n(10:00 release days in F) = 0`. A 10:00 calendar (ISM, consumer sentiment, JOLTS, new-home sales and similar) is not acquired; it must not be inferred from the 08:30 table.
- **Code.** **match** (input). `family_levels.py::load_red_folder` returns `{"0830", "fomc", "1000": set()}` and sets `release_1000 = False` for every session; `level_fixtures` records "no 10:00 table". Verdict stands until a 10:00 release table exists in the inventory.

### R-J21 — Market conditions and expectations · pass 0.0386 (rescored 2026-09-10)

### R-J22 — Failure protocol · pass 0.1236 (rescored 2026-09-10)

- **Source.** `[TBR p.37]`: "No Rejection Signature: When price reaches the projection levels during the 9:40-9:50 window but shows no rejection candles or orderblocks; Continuous Momentum: Price continues through projection levels with strong momentum and volume; Extended Bodies: Instead of wicks at projection levels, candles show extended bodies in the direction of the trend; … Missing the 9:40-9:50 Window; Extended Moves: Price moves beyond expected extensions without respecting time-based pivots; Premature Reversals … 3-Strike Rule: If price attempts to reverse 3 times at a level without follow-through, consider the setup failed; Volume Divergence: When price reaches critical levels but without corresponding volume signature". The page says "projection levels" (plural) and "a level": the mean-reversal area, ±0.5 and the 1.33–1.66 area on whichever side the session reached (the p.9–10 and p.21 charts).
- **Procedure.** On 3m candles (the entry-model timeframe) at the deepest projection level the session reached on the swept side (either side; the ladder of P3-01), after the first touch: (1) *rejection signature* = an R-J18 OB or rejection block forms within 15 min (named) — absent → `no_rejection = 1`; (2) *extended bodies* = the candle that touches the level has body/range ≥ 0.6 (source-unspecified threshold, named) in the break direction; (3) *continuous momentum* = `b.c1` beyond the level + δ with `h=15` hold; (4) *window violation* = first touch outside `bin.0940-0950`; (5) *3-strike* = three touches `t2` of the level each followed by a return < 0.25·R (named) and no G-default reject; (6) *volume divergence* = the touching candle's volume < the 14-period SMA (the only volume baseline the manual prints, `[TBR p.35]`); *extended moves* = `b.c1` beyond 1.66 + δ (the outer expected extension). Session label = failed if any of (1)–(6); then `switched_to_single_break = 1` if the session's path class is single-side and the edge holds.
- **Constants.** Printed: 3 strikes; 9:40–9:50; 14-period volume average (p.35). Source-unspecified: body ratio, attempt tolerance, momentum hold, the overshoot δ.
- **Different object if.** Failure is inferred from the outcome (price did not reverse) instead of from the signatures the page lists, or the level is fixed to the path side's ±0.5 (the session may have stopped at 0.2·W or run to 1.33), or one side only.
- **Variants (named, not faithful).** Width base `w.69` (faithful) | `w.ev` | `w.london` | `w.own`; level ±0.5 | ±1.33 | ±1.66 | band 1.33–1.66 | overshoot δ — each side (RULES C3).
- **Fixture.** Level 80.0 (−0.5) touched at 09:44, 09:47, 09:49 (three 3m bars with lows 79.75, 79.75, 80.00); returns after each: 3.0, 2.5, 1.5 pts with R = 20 → all < 5.0 → `strikes = 3` → `three_strike = 1`. Touching bar body 4.5 / range 6.0 = 0.75 ≥ 0.6 → `extended_body = 1`. High-side mirror: +0.5 = 120.0 touched three times (highs 120.25, 120.25, 120.0), returns 3.0 / 2.5 / 1.5 → `three_strike = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** `family_recipes.py` sets `j22_three_strike` from `formulas_jumbo.py::j22_three_strike` on 1m AM bars at `m05_low` when the path class is low-only, else `m05_high` (2-tick touch tolerance; `STRIKE_FRAC = 0.25`, named; rate 0.1236); `j22_extended_body` (`BODY_FRAC = 0.6`, named), `j22_window_violation`, `j22_volume_divergence` and `j22_failed` exist but are not in the predicate; no ladder / band level, no overshoot, no switched-to-single-break row.

### R-A01 — Balance-rule fade · pass 0.0788 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[AMT1 p.9]`: "Rule one, balance stays balanced until proven otherwise. Price inside a balance tends to remain inside it, and the extremes of that balance hold as support and resistance. Fade the edges back toward the POC until the market shows you acceptance outside." `[AMT1 p.5]`: "On any fixed range volume profile, set Value Area Volume to 70." `[MAMT p.4]`: "Stop using VAH and VAL as levels … they are outputs that move every time volume prints." `[ABS p.7]`: "Previous day's VAH and VAL, more usable, because that day is closed and the level is no longer moving under you." `[AMT1 p.13–14]`: "Inside value fade the edges." Figures: `[AMT1 p.9]` rule-one chart draws the balance extremes as two bands (top and bottom of the profile) with the touches circled on both and the rotation running edge to edge; `[MAMT p.5, p.8, p.10]` draw every failed auction as a red dashed box at an excursion beyond VAH or beyond VAL that comes back inside; `[AMT1 p.5]` draws the value area as a shaded box between the VAH and VAL lines.
- **Procedure.** (1) Balance = the prior session's RTH 09:30–16:00 trade profile (`value.vp.rth.trade`, 1-tick bins, POC = max-volume price, VA = 70% of volume expanded from the POC by the larger neighbour; `family_open.py::value_area`). Fixed at 16:00 of the prior session (`known_at`). (2) Precondition: the session is inside the balance at the touch time (no `b.c1` outside VAH/VAL with `h=30` hold since 09:30). (3) Level = VAH for shorts / VAL for longs; event = touch `t2`, then G-default reject (r = 0.5·R with R = VAH − VAL, k = 15, no `b.c1` first). (4) Target = POC: touch `t2` of POC within 60 min of the reject (the manual prints no horizon; 60 min is the wiki default, named). (5) Invalidation = acceptance outside: `b.c1` beyond the edge with `h=30`. (6) Filter rows: day type normal / neutral (`label.amt.day` from R-A10) and the tape at the edge (`flow.absorption.A` at VAH/VAL; blocked-tape rows stay unscored). (7) The general failed-auction share of `[MAMT p.5]` ("four times out of five, stepping back in"), its own denominator: every excursion beyond either edge (`b.wick`) that prints a 1m close back inside the VA before any `h=30` hold outside; both sides pooled and split.
- **Constants.** Printed: 70% value area; "roughly 80% … four times out of five" `[MAMT p.5]` as the claim to test. Source-unspecified: reject depth, hold window, POC-reach horizon, the thickness of the band the charts draw around VAH / VAL (the touch tolerance stays a grid value t0 / t2 / tR).
- **Different object if.** The current session's developing VA is used (`[MAMT p.4]` and `[ABS p.7]` call it lagging; a named row only), or a 40% VA is used (`[C3 p.7]` "Set the value area at 40% instead of 70% for intraday work", a named swap), or the touch is counted without the inside-balance precondition, or only one edge is scored (the charts draw both).
- **Fixture.** Session 2024-01-03, prior RTH profile (2024-01-02): POC 16700.0, VAL 16685.0, VAH 16779.75 (`prior_rth_trade_vp_F`). R = 94.75; reject depth 0.5·R = 47.375. 09:30 open 16610.0 is below VAL, so the inside-balance precondition fails at the open → `a01_eligible = 0` for 2024-01-03. Synthetic: VAL 100, VAH 120, POC 112, touch of VAL at 09:47 (low 99.75), 1m close 110.5 at 09:58 (≥ 100 + 10 = 110 ✓, no 1m close < 100 first) → `val_reject = 1`; POC touched 10:05 → `poc_reach_60 = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "G-default reject at prior VAL/VAH"): `formulas_jumbo.py::a01_fade` scores the G-default reject at VAL (side −1) and at VAH (side +1) with R = VAH − VAL and computes `inside` (no held break since the window start) and `poc_reach_60`; `family_recipes.py` sets `a01_fade = val_reject or vah_reject` and `_preds.a01` scores that flag alone, so the inside-balance precondition and the POC reach are computed but not in the predicate; the step-(7) excursion-return share is not computed. Earlier departure kept on record: `family_value.py::build_value_table` `kz` compares the AM extreme to the same session's full-RTH VA (lookahead).

### R-A02 — Ledge continuation · pass 0.6229 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[AMT1 p.9]`: "Rule two, out of balance the ledges carry the move. When price leaves a balance, the ledges of the prior balance are what hold for the move to continue. The level that was support becomes the resistance that fuels the next leg, and the reverse on the way up." `[VP2 p.4]`: "THE LEDGE: The lines themselves, the extremes of the shelf. The exact price where the build-up starts or the fade-away begins." `[VP2 p.5]`: "A ledge does not move." `[MAMT p.12]`: "Break out of balance and retest the broken boundary: enter in the direction of the break."
- **Procedure.** (1) Balance and ledge: on the prior RTH trade profile, a *shelf* = a maximal run of adjacent 1-tick bins each ≥ the profile's median bin volume (the wiki's named rule; the lesson prints no ratio); its *ledges* = the first and last price of that run. Ledges exist on both sides of the POC — `[VP2 p.5]` draws "four ledges around the POC" (two above, two below) as lines at the profile's volume steps — and the lesson carries two readings of "shelf": the text's body between the lines (`[VP2 p.4]` "everything in between the lines") and the figures' thin band at the transition (`[VP2 p.4]` "Shelfs" arrows on bands a few points tall; `[MATH p.13]` a shelf drawn as a band about ten points tall with the trade at it); the band thickness is unprinted, so the touch tolerance at a ledge is a grid value. The VA edges (VAH/VAL) are the fallback ledge set when the shelf rule yields none and are a different object when used alone (`[VP2 p.5]` "a ledge does not move", VAH / VAL drift). (2) Out-of-balance state: `b.c1` beyond the ledge (or VA edge) with `h=30`. (3) Retest: after the hold, first touch `t2` of the broken ledge from the outside. (4) Hold: G-default reject at the ledge in the break direction (r = 0.5·R, R = shelf height or VA height, k = 15) and no `b.c1` back inside within 30 min. (5) Continuation: touch `t2` of the next node beyond (next shelf ledge, prior H/L, or the composite HVN) by 16:00. Invalidation = `b.c1` back inside the balance.
- **Constants.** Printed: none numeric. Source-unspecified: the shelf detection rule, hold window, continuation horizon.
- **Different object if.** The ledge is replaced by the developing VA edge (moves under the test), or the retest is counted from inside the balance (that is R-A01's fade).
- **Fixture.** Prior profile bins (volume): 99.75:40, 100.00:210, 100.25:260, 100.50:240, 100.75:35 → median over the profile 120 (given); shelf = 100.00–100.50; ledges 100.00 and 100.50. Break: 1m closes 100.75 … 101.5 for 30 min (`b.c1` above 100.50 + hold) → out of balance. Retest at 10:20 low 100.5 (touch `t2`), 1m close 101.0 at 10:29 ≥ 100.5 + 0.5·0.5 = 100.75 ✓ → `ledge_retest_hold = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "AM prints at the prior VAL/VAH ledge"): `family_tape.py` sets `a02_ledge_hold` = any AM print within 2 ticks of `ledge`, where `ledge` = the prior VAL when the 09:30 open sits in the lower half of the VA, else the prior VAH — the VA edge stands in for the ledge (the different object named above), one side is picked by the open, and there is no break → retest → hold sequence. `formulas_jumbo.py::a02_shelves` / `a02_ledge_retest_hold` implement the shelf run and the break-hold-retest-reject sequence but are not called by the scorer. Earlier departure kept on record: `value.kz` (`family_value.py`) compares the AM extreme to the same session's RTH VA (lookahead).

### R-A03 — Failed-auction traverse (loose 80%) · pass 0.0634 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[AMT1 p.7]`: "When the market breaks out of value and later trades back into a prior value area, around 80% of the time it traverses that value in the opposite direction … once price re-enters and holds inside value, expect the full traverse." `[AMTL p.9]`: "80 percent that price runs all the way to the far extreme, 20 percent that it simply ranges inside instead." `[AMTL p.8]`: "If price reaches that lower area and rejects it, coming all the way back up rather than accepting into it, that's a failed auction." `[ABS p.12]`: "roughly 72 to 80 percent of the time price comes back into balance rather than continuing to trend away from it."
- **Procedure.** (1) Prior VA = prior RTH trade profile VAL/VAH (fixed). (2) Breakout: price outside the VA (session opens outside, or `b.c1` beyond an edge with `h=30` during RTH). (3) Re-entry: `b.c1` back inside through the same edge, then hold inside (no `b.c1` back out) for 30 min (the manual says "re-enters and holds"; the 30 min is the G-default hold, named). (4) Traverse: touch `t2` of the opposite edge by 16:00 (the manual prints no horizon). (5) Denominator: re-entries with the hold. Invalidation = `b.c1` back out before the traverse.
- **Constants.** Printed: 80%; 72–80% (ABS). Source-unspecified: hold window, horizon.
- **Different object if.** Two consecutive 30-minute periods inside are required (that is the strict rule, R-A04), or the entry side is not required to be a prior breakout (a plain open-inside-value traverse).
- **Fixture.** Session 2024-01-03: open 16610.0 < VAL 16685.0 (outside); the AM path is low-only (`path_class` low-only, high 16649.75 max shown by the 6–9 H) so no `b.c1` above 16685 in RTH → `reentry = 0`, traverse not evaluated. Synthetic: VAL 100, VAH 120; open 96; 1m close 100.5 at 10:03 (re-entry), closes stay ≥ 100 to 10:33 (hold) → `reentry_hold = 1`; high ≥ 119.5 at 13:40 → `traverse = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "failed-auction re-entry then traverse to the opposite VA edge"): `formulas_jumbo.py::a03_reentry_traverse` scores the re-entry (`b.c1` back through the edge), the `h=30` hold and the traverse to the opposite edge when the 09:30 open is below VAL (up case) or above VAH (down case); a breakout during RTH from an inside open returns zeros, so step (2)'s second branch is not scored; `_preds.a03` = `a03_traverse`.

### R-A05 — POC tell · pass 0.3369 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[AMTL p.9]`: "Inside that balance there is a POC. If price keeps coming in and constantly fails to break through it and hold, that's a clear sign you're most likely getting the 20 percent case, a session that just chops inside fair value. But if buyers push aggressively through POC, maybe with a retest that holds, that's the tell you're more likely seeing the 80 percent case play out." `[RTVP p.5]`: "If price fails to break above POC, retesting it again and again without holding above, the more likely outcome is a full trip back down to value area low. If instead price retests aggressively and then pushes through POC with real aggression, value area high becomes the more likely target."
- **Procedure.** (1) POC of the prior RTH trade profile (fixed). (2) After the R-A03 re-entry (or an open inside value), watch the POC: *chop case* = ≥ 2 touches `t2` of the POC without a `b.c1` through it that holds `h=30`; *traverse case* = `b.c1` through the POC, then a touch `t2` retest, then G-default reject in the through direction (retest hold). (3) Outcome: touch `t2` of the far edge (VAH if through upward / VAL) by 16:00 vs touch of the near edge; split by case. `known_at` = the second failed touch or the retest hold.
- **Constants.** Printed: "again and again" (≥ 2 named). Source-unspecified: hold window, horizon.
- **Different object if.** The POC is the developing session POC (moves), or the "through" is a wick instead of a close.
- **Fixture.** Prior POC 112 (VAL 100, VAH 120). Touches of 112 at 10:10 (high 112.25, close 110), 10:31 (high 112.0, close 109.5), no 1m close > 112 by 11:00 → `poc_case = chop`; low ≤ 100.5 at 11:42 → `near_edge_reach = 1`, `far_edge_reach = 0`. Dated input: 2024-03-13 prior POC 18218.0, VAL 18078.5, VAH 18241.0, open 18181.25 inside value.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "POC chop (two touches, no through-and-hold)"): `formulas_jumbo.py::a05_poc_tell` counts `t2` touches of the POC, sets the through side from the first close versus the POC, labels *traverse* on a held `b.c1` through the POC or a far-side retest reject and *chop* on ≥ 2 touches without either, and prints near / far edge reach; `_preds.a05` = `a05_poc_chop`, so the traverse case and the edge-reach split are computed but not scored.

### R-A06 — MAMT Failed Auction setup · pass 0.4900 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[MAMT p.9]`: "The setup requires balance, a break out of it, and then price actually travelling to and tagging a prior balance, an older one, at its POC. When it hits that prior POC and instantly rejects, refusing to accept it, there is an 80% statistic behind what happens next: price travels back to the boundary of the balance it broke from in the first place, its established balance. Reject from above a prior POC and the target is that established balance's VAH. Reject from below and the target is its VAL." `[MAMT p.26]`: "Balance, break, and a tag of a PRIOR balance's POC, not just any retest. An instant rejection at that POC, not acceptance of it." Figures: `[MAMT p.10]` draws both directions (break down → tag the prior balance below from above → reject → back up to the far boundary; break up → tag the prior balance above from below → reject → back down); `[MAMT p.11]` chart 1 marks "vah = target" at the top of the established balance after a downside break and a tag of the grey prior-balance box below it. `[AMTL p.10]`: "It dipped meaningfully into the previous balance, deep enough that a less patient read might have called it accepted. It still failed to actually hold" — the rejection can come after a deep dip, not only instantly.
- **Procedure.** (1) Established balance = the prior session's RTH trade profile (VAH/VAL/POC). (2) Older balance = the most recent earlier session whose RTH profile POC has not been traded through since (a naked POC, `[VP2 p.6]`), on the side of the break. (3) Sequence: `b.c1` beyond the established VA edge with `h=30` (break) → touch `t2` of the older POC (tag) → instant rejection = 1m close back ≥ 0.5·R toward the established balance within k = 5 min with no `b.c1` through the older POC first (R = |older POC − established edge|). (4) Target = the *far* boundary of the established balance: its VAH when the break was downward and the tag came from above the older POC (the p.11 "vah = target" line), its VAL for the upward mirror (p.10 right panel); outcome = touch `t2` by 16:00. (5) Tag depth is a printed column, not a filter: `t2` touch of the older POC (MAMT "instant"), entry into the older VA, or a deep dip (≥ 0.5 × the older VA height, named) that still rejects (`[AMTL p.10]`); the k = 5 "instant" window is the MAMT reading only. Invalidation = `b.c1` through the older POC with `h=30` (acceptance).
- **Constants.** Printed: 80%. Source-unspecified: the "instant" window (k = 5 is `RULES.md`'s named value), the deep-dip depth, the naked-POC lookback.
- **Different object if.** The tag is of any level (that is chart 2 of `[MAMT p.11]`, a trend-continuation retest), or the target is the older balance's edge instead of the established balance's edge.
- **Fixture.** Established balance VAL 100 / VAH 120; older naked POC 92 (from 3 sessions back). Break: 1m closes < 100 from 10:00 with hold to 10:30. Tag: low 91.75 at 10:52 (within 2 ticks of 92); 1m close 96.5 at 10:56 ≥ 92 + 0.5·8 = 96 ✓ within 5 min → `fa_setup = 1`; high ≥ 99.5 at 12:10 → `target_val_reach = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "RTH POC untraded overnight (naked POC)"): `family_tape.py` sets `a06_naked_poc` = no overnight print within 1 tick of the prior RTH POC and `_preds.a06` scores that flag alone — a naked-POC precondition, not the setup. `formulas_jumbo.py::a06_failed_auction` implements break → hold → tag → instant reject (k = 5) but sets `target = val` for a downside break and `vah` for an upside break — the *near* boundary — where the text and the p.11 chart target the *far* boundary (VAH after a downside break); it is not called by the scorer. No older-balance (naked POC) list, no tag-depth column.

### R-A07 — Break-retest continuation · pass 0.0155 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[RTVP p.6]`: "A continuation, in his framing, is price breaking out of the range, coming back into it for a retest, and then continuing in the direction of the original break." `[RTVP p.8]`: "if price opens and comes down to retest the lower shelf and buyers hold it, the read is a long back toward the upper extreme, entry at the retest, target at the far shelf." `[MAMT p.12]`: "Break out of balance and retest the broken boundary: enter in the direction of the break." `[WIC p.5]`: "A short only becomes valid once price breaks that low, comes back up for a genuine retest, and then continues lower from there." `[WIC p.8]`: "Not the initial break, the retest that held." `[TRAP p.6]`: "wait for a breakout of the intraday range, then wait again for price to come back and retest it. Two waits, not one." Figures: `[TRAP p.6–7]` draw the intraday levels off the session open and balance as grey bands and mark the short at the retest of the broken band's edge; `[WIC p.8]` draws the balance boundaries as bands; `[MAMT p.12]` marks the three entries on both boundaries of a composite balance box.
- **Procedure.** (1) Boundary set: prior RTH VAH/VAL, prior RTH shelf ledges (R-A02 rule), IB high/low (09:30–10:30 1s H/L). (2) Break: `b.c1` beyond the boundary with `h=30`. (3) Retest: first touch `t2` of the boundary from the outside after the hold. (4) Hold: G-default reject in the break direction and no `b.c1` back inside within 30 min. (5) Reach: touch `t2` of the next value area (next shelf, prior H/L, or `lvl.owed.nearest`) by 16:00. Filter: HTF direction agrees = the prior-session RTH close vs its VA (above → longs only; named per `[WIC p.10]` "the higher timeframe read is a bias"). Invalidation = `b.c1` back inside.
- **Constants.** Printed: none. Source-unspecified: hold window, next-value horizon.
- **Different object if.** The retest is a wick touch without the prior `h=30` hold (that is a fail-back candidate, `fail_prior.rth`), or the direction filter is dropped (that is R-A02 without OF-A9).
- **Fixture.** IB 09:30–10:30 H 110 / L 100. 1m closes > 110 from 10:41 to 11:11 (break + hold). Retest: low 110.25 at 11:30 (within 2 ticks of 110), 1m close 113.5 at 11:38 ≥ 110 + 0.5·10 = 115? No (113.5 < 115) → wait; close 115.25 at 11:44 ✓ within 15 min of the touch → `retest_hold = 1`. Next value = prior RTH H 118: high 118.0 at 13:02 → `next_value_reach = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "IB break, retest, hold"): `family_recipes.py` sets `a07_ib_retest` from `formulas_jumbo.py::a07_break_retest` called for the IB high (break side +1) and the IB low (−1): `b.c1` + `h=30`, retest touch, G-default reject in the break direction, no held `b.c1` back inside; the prior VAH / VAL and shelf-ledge boundaries of step (1), the next-value reach and the HTF-direction filter are not scored.

### R-A11 — Profile-shape gate · pass 0.0000 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[AMT1 p.6]`: "D BALANCED: A two sided auction. Fat middle, symmetric … Fade the edges back toward the POC. P SHORT COVERING: Fat top, thin tail below … Expect balance after. b LONG LIQUIDATION: Fat bottom, thin tail above … Same warning, reversed. B DOUBLE DISTRIBUTION: Two separate balances joined by a thin bridge. Treat each as its own value area, the bridge is the line in the sand." `[RTVP p.9]`: "A trending profile … a shape with no real bell curve, just a long, narrow spread of volume. His instruction for this shape is the whole point of including it: when you see one, get off." `[MAMT p.7]`: "A P day has its bulk of volume sitting toward the top of the balance … and it resolves with price breaking down out of the bottom. A B day is the mirror of it." `[MAMT p.8]`: "A D day is neither." Figures: `[AMT1 p.6]` draws D / P / b / B with the thin tail or bridge in red (P: tail below; b: tail above; B: bridge in the middle); `[RTVP p.10–11]` draws the P bulge at the top with the drive from below and the B bulge at the bottom with the drive from above, the range at the bulge read for continuation; `[MAMT p.7]` draws the P day resolving *down* out of the box at the top and the B day resolving *up* — the opposite resolution to RTVP's, so each author's claim is scored on its own.
- **Procedure.** (1) Profile = prior RTH trade profile (default; overnight 18:00–09:30 and the 5-session composite as named rows), 1-tick bins. (2) Shape rule (the lessons draw the shapes and print no thresholds; the wiki's named rule): split the VA into thirds by price; top-third share `s_t`, bottom-third `s_b`, middle `s_m` of VA volume; *P* if `s_t ≥ 0.45` and `s_b ≤ 0.20`; *b* mirror; *double* if two local maxima ≥ 1.5 × the median bin exist with a bridge bin ≤ 0.5 × median between them; *trending* if VA width ≥ 0.7 × range and no bin ≥ 1.5 × median; *D* otherwise. (3) Outcomes: next session's path class (`sessions.py::path_class_from_closes`) and prior-VA edge-reject rate by shape *and by direction* (up-break vs down-break share), P and b both scored (the mirror is drawn on every page), so that the three claims print separately: AMT1 "expect balance after" (next-session neither / normal share), RTVP "continuation in the drive direction" (P → high-only, b → low-only share), MAMT "P resolves down, B resolves up" (P → low-only, b → high-only share); trending share = "no trade" share.
- **Constants.** Printed: none. Source-unspecified: every threshold above.
- **Different object if.** The shape is read from the 6–9 box (the retained `vp_shape_69` is that; it returns "double" for every fixture session because the box is 1m-OHLC-built and the peak test saturates, `CONSTRUCTION_AUDIT.md` fail row), or from the developing session (`[AMT1 p.13]`: "A D shape at 11am can be a trend day by 2pm").
- **Fixture.** VA bins (volume, low→high, 9 bins): 30, 40, 45, 60, 70, 65, 200, 210, 180 → thirds: bottom 115, middle 195, top 590 of 900 → `s_t = 0.656 ≥ 0.45`, `s_b = 0.128 ≤ 0.20` → `shape = P`. Dated: `vp_shape_69` = "double" on 2024-01-03, 2024-03-13 and 2025-09-15 (saturated).
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "prior RTH P-shape then single-break path", rate 0.0000): `family_recipes.py` sets `a11_prior_p` = `vp_p_shape(vols) == "P"` and a single-side path class of either direction — b is not scored and the up / down split that separates the three authors' claims is absent; `formulas_jumbo.py::a11_vp_shape` carries the named thresholds (`VA_P_TOP` 0.45, `VA_P_BOT` 0.20, `NODE_HVN` 1.5, `NODE_LVN` 0.5, `TREND_VA_FRAC` 0.7). Earlier departure kept on record: `vp_shape_69` (6–9 OHLC box) saturates at "double".

### R-A12 — Overnight inventory and LVN · pass 0.1144 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[MAMT p.14]`: "Overnight inventory … From 6pm the previous day to 9:30am New York open … Whether price nets long or short across that window matters because it tends to carry into the open: net long overnight, the path of least resistance at 9:30 is higher, net short, it's lower … The tool for judging whether that overnight bias survives the open is the low volume node inside the overnight profile, drawn from 6pm to 9:30. One clean distribution and the net environment usually just continues. A double distribution and the low volume node between the two humps becomes the decision point, the level that gets 'respected' if the open continues the overnight direction, or 'disrespected' if it doesn't. The overnight shelf reads the same way: hold it and price likely stays inside the overnight range, break it with real aggression and price is more likely heading back to test the overnight extreme." Figure `[MAMT p.14]`: the overnight profile with "SHELF" drawn as a box at the edge of the upper hump and "LVN" as a box spanning the low-volume stretch between the two humps (both bands, not lines), a red "POC alignment" line where the RTH POC sits inside the LVN box, the RTH price action touching the LVN box (red circles) and the shelf box (green circles) before breaking above; caption "Net long from 6pm. The shelf and the LVN both hold".
- **Procedure.** (1) Overnight profile = trades 18:00 → 09:30 (`value.vp.on`), 1-tick bins. (2) Net inventory: sign of Σ(aggressive buy − aggressive sell) over 18:00–09:30 from MBP-1 (FL-11; the bar proxy `flow.cvd.ohlc` sign is a named variant). The tape-trust gate applies: `flow.cvd.trade` is tape-trusted "no" in `FINDINGS.md`, so the delta sign stays unscored; the object is the profile. (3) Double distribution test: two local maxima ≥ 1.5 × median bin with a bridge bin ≤ 0.5 × median (wiki rule, source prints none); LVN = the band from the first to the last bridge bin ≤ 0.5 × median (the p.14 box), with the minimum-volume price as the line variant; shelf = the ledge band at the edge of the hump nearest the open (the p.14 box at the upper hump's edge), with the overnight VA edge as a named alternative; `poc_alignment` = the developing RTH POC inside the LVN band (drawn, not defined). The net-long case is the drawn one; the net-short mirror (shelf at the lower hump, open continuing down) is the same object. (4) Open read: from 09:30, *respected* = the LVN is touched `t2` and G-default-rejected (r = 0.5·R with R = distance between the two humps' POCs, k = 15); *disrespected* = `b.c1` through the LVN with `h=30`. (5) Outcomes: overnight extreme (ONH/ONL, `range.on.1800-0930`) touched by 12:00 after disrespect vs respect; open-direction agreement with the inventory sign (label only while the tape is untrusted).
- **Constants.** Printed: 6pm–9:30am. Source-unspecified: the distribution thresholds, hold windows.
- **Different object if.** The profile is the prior RTH profile (that is R-A01/A11), or the inventory sign comes from price change instead of aggressor delta.
- **Fixture.** Overnight bins: hump A POC 100 (300 lots), hump B POC 110 (280), bridge min at 105 (40) with median 120 → 40 ≤ 60 ✓ and 300, 280 ≥ 180 ✓ → `double = 1`, `lvn = 105`. Open 09:30 at 108; low 105.0 at 09:41 (touch), close 108.25 at 09:50 ≥ 105 + 0.5·10 = 110? No; close 110.5 at 09:55 ✓ → `respected = 1`. Dated: 2025-09-15 net overnight delta not scored (tape gate); `range.on` H/L touched: `onh_touch = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "09:30 open at an overnight LVN"): `family_tape.py` builds the overnight trade profile, takes `profile_nodes(...)["lvn"]` (single prices) and sets `a12_on_lvn` = the 09:30 open within 2 ticks of one of them; `_preds.a12` scores that flag. `formulas_jumbo.py::a12_double_lvn` / `a12_respected` implement the two-hump test, the LVN line and the respected (G-default reject) / disrespected (held `b.c1`) events but are not called; no LVN band, no shelf band, no POC alignment; the inventory sign stays gated (`a12_inventory_sign` returns None unless tape-trusted).

### R-A14 — TPO unfinished business · pass 1.0000 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[TPO p.5]`: "A single print is a single row of letters from one time period, with no overlap from any other period. Price moved through so fast that only one 30 minute window ever touched it … The market tends to come back and fill it, like a magnet. Mark fresh single prints as unfinished business." `[TPO p.6]`: "Excess forms at the edges of a TPO profile, when two or more rows of the same letter print at the extreme." `[TPO p.7]`: "A poor high or poor low forms when the market shows weak rejection from a level … Poor highs and lows usually appear as a single TPO tail." `[TPO p.9]`: "Excess marks where an argument ended. Expect those extremes to hold on first test." Figures: `[TPO p.5]` marks the single print as a run of seven "G"-only rows *inside* the profile between two distributions, not at an extreme; `[TPO p.6]` marks "Excess Low" at five rows of "I" alone at the bottom and "Excess High" at "A" stacked at the top — both sides; `[TPO p.7]` marks the "Poor Low" at bottom rows two letters wide ("Q R") with no single-letter tail, so the drawn poor extreme is the ≥ 2-periods-at-the-extreme reading; `[TPO p.4]` and `[TPO p.9]` read premium / discount against the TPO value area ("Above it, price is trading at a premium … Below it … at a discount"). The p.5 "HOW TO USE THEM" sentence is clipped in the PDF itself after "They are targets when price is moving toward them, and". `[C3 p.6]`: "Single prints from the TPO chart and balances … are the destinations price trades between."
- **Procedure.** (1) TPO profile of the prior RTH: 30-minute periods A (09:30–10:00) … M (15:30–16:00), rows = 1-point price rows (`[TPO p.3]` prints 30 min; the row size is source-unspecified, 1 point is the code's value). A period covers a row if the period's [low, high] spans it. (2) Single print = a row covered by exactly one period and not at the profile's top or bottom row. (3) Excess = at the profile high (low), ≥ 2 rows covered only by the same single period (a tail of length ≥ 2). (4) Poor extreme = the top (bottom) row covered by ≥ 2 periods (the p.7 drawing), or a tail of exactly 1 row (the lesson's "single TPO tail" phrase for NQ); both readings are printed as named rows, never pooled — every profile's extreme row falls into exactly one of {≥ 2 periods, 1-row tail, ≥ 2-row tail}, so a pooled poor-or-excess presence flag is always true. (5) Outcomes next session 09:30–16:00: single-print *fill* = touch `t2` of every row of the single-print run (repair = a later 30-minute period covers it); poor-extreme *revisit* = touch `t2` of the extreme; excess *hold on first test* = first touch `t2` of the excess extreme followed by a G-default reject (R = tail length).
- **Constants.** Printed: 30-minute letters; "two or more rows" for excess. Source-unspecified: row size, fill tolerance.
- **Different object if.** The profile is built from trades visited (that is `tpo_trade_visited`, a coverage flag), or the extreme test ignores which period printed it.
- **Fixture.** Prior RTH periods: A [100, 104], B [103, 108], C [107, 115], D [112, 114], E [113, 114] … rows 109–111 covered only by C → `single = [109, 111]`; profile high 115 covered by C only, one row (114 is covered by C, D, E) → tail length 1 → `poor_high = 1` (single-TPO-tail reading), `excess_high = 0`. Next session low 108.75 at 10:12 → all rows 109–111 touched → `single_fill = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "TPO poor extreme or excess hold", rate 1.0000): `_preds.a14` = `tpo_poor or tpo_excess_hold or tpo_single_fill`; `formulas_jumbo.py::a14_tpo` builds singles (interior rows covered by one period), `poor_high/low` = extreme row covered by ≥ 2 periods *or* a 1-row tail, `excess_high/low` = a tail ≥ 2 rows, on both sides — so poor ∨ excess holds for every profile by construction and the rate 1.0 measures nothing; the two poor readings are pooled with OR instead of printed separately; `a14_single_fill` exists but the next-session fill / revisit / hold-on-first-test outcomes are not scored. Earlier departure kept on record: `family_gap.py::_tpo_poor` and `family_levels.py` `tpo_single_fill` / `tpo_excess_hold` are current-session extreme flags, not the prior profile tested next day.

### R-A17 — Two-transition extremes · pass 0.0958 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[MATH p.13]`: "A low volume node needs a second transition after it, a return to balance, to actually count as one rather than just a tail, a taper of volume trailing off with nothing behind it. Same rule for a shelf versus a ledge: a shelf is volume gradually tapering into the next transition, a ledge is volume cutting off instantly with no taper at all. Mark either one without a second transition behind it and you're not looking at a real extreme, you're looking at noise." `[MATH p.15]`: "My low volume nodes, ledges and shelves all have a second transition behind them, not just a taper I'm calling an extreme."
- **Procedure.** (1) On the prior RTH (or composite) profile, candidate LVN = a local minimum bin ≤ 0.5 × median (wiki rule). (2) *Second transition* = beyond the LVN (away from the current balance) there exists a bin run ≥ the median (a return to balance) within the profile; *tail* = volume declines monotonically to the profile edge with no such run. (3) Ledge vs shelf: a cutoff is a ledge if the bin volume drops by ≥ 3× between adjacent bins (named), a shelf if the drop is spread over ≥ 3 bins. (4) Event: touch `t2` of the LVN / ledge, G-default reject (R = the balance height). (5) Score: reject rate with vs without the second transition.
- **Constants.** Printed: none. Source-unspecified: 0.5×, 3×, run length.
- **Different object if.** VAH/VAL are used as the extremes (`[MATH p.13]`: "arbitrary, they shift as volume builds").
- **Fixture.** Bins (low→high): 150, 160, 40, 30, 140, 155 (median 145). LVN at bin 4 (30 ≤ 72.5 ✓); beyond it bins 5–6 ≥ 145 → `second_transition = 1`. Second profile: 150, 160, 40, 30, 20, 10 → no run ≥ median beyond the minimum → `tail = 1`, not an extreme.
- **Code.** **match** Rescored 2026-09-10 (`RULES_SCORES.md` event "second volume transition in the RTH trade profile"): `family_tape.py` sets `a17_second_tx` = `formulas_jumbo.py::a17_second_transition` on the RTH trade profile's bins (deepest LVN ≤ 0.5 × median; a bin ≥ the median beyond it on the lighter side = second transition, a monotone decline = tail); the ledge-vs-shelf split and the reject-at-the-level outcome are not scored (`_preds.a17` = the presence flag).

### R-A18 — C3 balance-position bias · pass 0.6198 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[C3 p.6]`: "Price can only do three things: stay in a balance, leave a balance, or return to a previous one … INSIDE A BALANCE: If your level sits inside a balance, avoid trading it. AT THE EXTREMES: Trade levels at the edges of balance or outside it … Rejection from a balance with an unfilled single print above: bullish bias. Acceptance below a balance: bearish bias." `[C3 p.7]`: "Treat the thesis like a box. The bias has a validity zone."
- **Procedure.** (1) Balance = prior RTH VA; single prints from R-A14 (unfilled = not touched since they printed). (2) Bullish case: touch `t2` of VAL (or the balance low) with a G-default reject, and an unfilled single print above the balance exists → bias long; outcome = touch `t2` of the lowest row of that single print by 16:00. (3) Bearish case: `b.c1` below VAL with `h=30` (acceptance) → bias short; outcome = touch of the nearest single print / poor low below (if any), else the prior RTH low. (3b) Mirrors — the source prints one example each way and the mirrors are the same object, printed as named rows: rejection at VAH (or the balance high) with an unfilled single print below → bias short, outcome the highest row of that single; acceptance above VAH (`b.c1` + `h=30`) → bias long, outcome the nearest single / poor high above, else the prior RTH high. (4) Levels inside the balance are excluded from the trigger set (FL).
- **Constants.** Printed: none. Source-unspecified: hold window, horizon.
- **Different object if.** The single print is inside the balance (excluded by the source), or acceptance is a wick instead of a held close.

- **Source.** `[VWAP p.3]`: "The lines above and below are standard deviation bands. Run plus and minus 2 and 2.5, you can add 3 but price rarely touches it." Its chart draws the median with one band above and one below, the author's hand labels reading "−2" on the upper band, "0" on the median and "+2" on the lower band, and the bands run through the overnight hours (the time axis spans 08:00 PM to 04:00 AM). `[VWAP p.4]`: "One: the trades worth taking live beyond the 1 band and ideally at the 2, deal with at least the 1. Two: only trade VWAP extremes WITH absorption, never on the touch alone … If there is no confirmation, or the draw beyond the band is clearly still higher or lower, do not trade against it." `[VWAP p.8]` settings screenshot: Inputs tab — Anchor Period "Session", Source "(H + L + C)/3", Bands Calculation Mode "Standard Deviation", Bands Multiplier #1 = 1 and #2 = 2 ticked, #3 unticked; Style tab — VWAP, Upper / Lower Band #1 and #2 ticked, band fills off (the caption's "plus and minus 1, 2 and 2.5" is text; the screenshot enables 1 and 2 only). `[VWAP p.3]`: "the VWAP median is the POC of the session so far". `[VWAP p.7]`: "The session VWAP resets every day."

(2) Event, both sides: touch `t2` of the upper band (premium, fade short) or the lower band (discount, fade long) at m = 2 (m = 1 as the "at least" row); then `flow.absorption.A` at that band price within 15 min (aggressive volume toward the band ≥ frozen q90 in 2 min at the band ±2 ticks, advance ≤ 2 ticks). (3) Filter: `lvl.owed.nearest` beyond the band not still open (no untouched draw within 1·σ beyond the band; named distance). (4) Outcome: touch `t2` of the VWAP median within 60 min (named) after the touch, split touch+absorption vs touch alone, per side. `known_at` = bar close.

(3) Breakout grade: at `b.c1` beyond a level, CVD slope over the last 5 min > 0 (real) vs ≤ 0 (fakeout). (4) Absorption zone: price range ≤ 2 ticks over 2 min while |ΔCVD| ≥ q90 (this is `flow.absorption.A`'s definition on the CVD axis).

### R-F04 — Footprint stacked-imbalance magnet · pass 0.7311 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[FP8 p.4]`: "you do not compare them straight across, you compare them diagonally … Compare the 56 ask to the 5 bid one tick below." `[FP8 p.5]`: "Most platforms mark them when one side is 3x to 4x larger than the diagonal opposite … 3x to 4x is the standard flag." (The p.5 figure highlights the asks 220 / 410 / 512 at 6663.75–6663.25 while the bids printed one tick below them read 4120 / 3980 / 2110, so the highlighted cells do not satisfy the 3×–4× diagonal rule; the figure is illustrative and the rule is the text.) `[FP8 p.6]`: "Three or more imbalances in a row build an unfinished auction, the market tends to return and finish it … Several buy imbalances in a column show aggressive buyers stepping up tick after tick. It marks a demand zone, price often revisits it and holds." `[FP8 p.7]`: "Two or three flagged imbalances in a row at a level is real pressure."
- **Procedure.** (1) Per 1m candle from MBP-1 trades: ask volume (aggressive buys) and bid volume (aggressive sells) per 1-tick price. (2) Buy imbalance at price p: ask(p) ≥ k × bid(p − 1 tick), k ∈ {3, 4} (both printed; 4 default as the code's value); sell imbalance at p: bid(p) ≥ k × ask(p + 1 tick); zero opposite volume → flag only if the side's volume ≥ 1 lot (named). (3) Stack = ≥ 3 consecutive prices with the same-side flag inside one candle (`flow.footprint.stack3`); zone = [lowest, highest] stacked price; `known_at` = candle close. (4) Revisit: a later touch `t2` of the zone (first time price leaves it by ≥ 4 ticks then returns; named). (5) Outcome: G-default reject at the zone in the imbalance direction (hold), by 16:00; overlap flag with `flow.absorption.A` at the same touch (must not be identical, `RULES.md`).
- **Constants.** Printed: 3×–4×; "three or more" in a row (the zone, p.6); "two or three flagged imbalances in a row at a level" (the at-level minimum, p.7); stacked buying "in a column" and stacked selling "stacked down" (both sides, p.6). Source-unspecified: leave distance, zero-volume handling.
- **Different object if.** The stack is counted over the whole AM profile instead of within one candle (that is `footprint_4x`, a session flag), or the diagonal is replaced by side-by-side.
- **Fixture.** Candle 10:03 ask/bid per tick: 100.00 ask 40 / bid 5; 100.25 ask 36 / bid 8; 100.50 ask 44 / bid 9; 100.75 ask 12 / bid 30. Buy imbalances: 100.25 (36 ≥ 4·5=20 ✓), 100.50 (44 ≥ 4·8=32 ✓), 100.75 (12 ≥ 4·9=36 ✗) — only 2 in a row → `stack3 = 0` at k=4; at k=3: 100.75 12 ≥ 27 ✗ → `stack3 = 0`. Second candle with 100.00/100.25/100.50 flagged → `stack3 = 1`, zone [100.00, 100.50]; revisit low 100.5 at 11:20 then close 103.0 at 11:31 (R = 0.5 → ≥ 100.75 ✓) → `revisit_hold = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** `mbp1_objects.py::footprint_4x` builds one AM-wide price ladder (09:30–12:00 aggregate, not per candle), flags 4× diagonals, and returns true if 3 adjacent same-side flags exist anywhere; no zone, no revisit, no hold. `footprint_stack3` is hard-set `True` in `_score_session`. The 2026-09-10 rescoring adds `formulas_flow.py::r_f04_candle_stack` (per-candle diagonal stack, k = 4) and wires `family_tape.py` `f04_stack_revisit` = that stack with a later AM trade back through the zone, OR-ed with the old AM-wide `footprint_4x`; no hold at the zone.

### R-F05 — Footprint absorption stack · pass 0.7573 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[FP9 p.4]`: "Bullish absorption: the candle is bullish but delta is negative, sellers are aggressive yet price still rises. Bearish absorption: the candle is bearish but delta is positive, buyers are aggressive yet price still falls." `[FP9 p.5]`: "A POC flip is that busiest price jumping to the other side of the candle … POC low in the candle then flipping high means the business moved up with conviction." (Its schematic draws two seven-row candles with the POC moving from the second-lowest row to the second-highest, ≈ 0.17 → 0.83 of the candle height, which both the halves and the thirds reading satisfy.) `[FP9 p.7]`: "Level, then absorption, then flip: Price reaches your level, delta disagrees with the candle, then the POC flips your way. That sequence is the full confirmation stack."
- **Procedure.** (1) Location = a level from the ledge / VA-edge / naked-POC set (R-A02, R-A06), touched `t2`. (2) Disagreement candle (1m; 2m and 5m named): at the level, close > open with Δ = Σbuy − Σsell < 0 (bullish absorption) or close < open with Δ > 0 (bearish). (3) Candle POC = max-volume price of the candle; position = (POC − low)/(high − low); flip = the next candle's POC position moves from the lower third to the upper third (bullish) or the reverse (wiki thirds rule; the lesson says "the other side of the candle", so halves are the named variant). (4) Outcomes: reversal ≥ 0.25·R (R = the balance height) within 15 min after (a) disagreement alone, (b) disagreement + flip.
- **Constants.** Printed: none numeric. Source-unspecified: thirds vs halves, the reversal size and window.
- **Different object if.** Delta is from 1m OHLC (`flow.cvd.ohlc`), or the flip is measured across non-adjacent candles.
- **Fixture.** Level VAL 100. Candle 10:07: open 100.25, close 100.75 (bullish), Δ = −180 (sell-aggressive) → `disagree_bull = 1`; POC 100.25 at position (100.25−100.0)/(101.0−100.0)=0.25 (lower third). Candle 10:08: POC 100.9, position 0.9 (upper third) → `poc_flip = 1`. Close 105.5 by 10:20 with R = 20 → 5.0 ≥ 0.25·20 ✓ → `reversal = 1`.
- **Code.** **match** No candle-delta or candle-POC function (`mbp1_objects.py` has session-level `vp_rth` only). The 2026-09-10 rescoring wires `family_tape.py` `f05_poc_flip` = the candle-vs-delta disagreement alone (`formulas_flow.py::r_f05_absorption_stack` `disagree_bull` / `disagree_bear`), no POC flip and no level tie.

### R-F06 — DOM absorption at a level · pass 0.0325 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[DOM6 p.3]`: "Big market orders hitting your level that do not push price through it. The aggression arrives, the level eats it, price reverses." `[DOM6 p.4]`: "Mark your levels first: Shelves, ledges, value edges, old highs and lows … Remember it can spread: Absorption is not always one price. 300 buy orders can get eaten across 3 ticks. Watch the zone, not just the single tick." `[DOM6 p.6]`: "Aggression into the level, no follow-through … Stacked size that stays … Delta agrees with the read: Positive delta with no upward progress means trapped buyers." `[DOM6 p.7]`: "Big volume with no movement is absorption, someone is there. Shrinking volume with no movement is exhaustion, nobody is left." `[DOM5 p.6]`: "FAST TAPE INTO A LEVEL: Aggression is committed. If the level still holds, that is absorption worth trading."
- **Procedure.** (1) Level set: prior RTH shelf ledges, VA edges, prior RTH H/L, Asia/London H/L (marked before the touch). (2) Part 1 `flow.absorption.A` at the level: over a 2-minute window ending at the touch, aggressive volume toward the level (MBP-1 aggressor side) ≥ the frozen q90 cut (buy 3,533 / sell 3,441 lots, discovery 2024-01-02→2024-11-15), price advance beyond the level ≤ 2 ticks (the source's "across 3 ticks" zone → `tick_tol` 3 as a named variant), then reversal ≥ 0.25·R (R = the level's own box width) within 15 min. (3) Part 3 delta agreeing: Σ aggressive buy − sell over the touch window has the sign of the aggressor with no progress (advance ≤ 2 ticks) → trapped. (4) Part 2 stacked size that stays and reloads: BBO reload at the touch = `flow.absorption.B` (blocked; fires every session), behind the touch = MBP-10 (not in the inventory). (5) Exhaustion split: the 2-minute aggressive volume at the stall < the prior 2-minute volume (shrinking) vs ≥ (big) — `[DOM6 p.7]`.
- **Constants.** Printed: 3 ticks (zone). Source-unspecified: the volume cut (q90 is the frozen discovery choice), window, reversal fraction.

Second tape with reversal to 16690 (67.5 ≥ 62.75 ✓) → `= 1`. Exhaustion split: prior window volume 4,100 > 3,600 → `shrinking = 1`.

(3) Outcome: reversal ≥ 0.25·R within 15 min.

### R-F08 — ABS four-check absorption · pass 0.4992 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[ABS p.3]`: "roughly 27 percent of all absorptions fail without what he calls a reward system, a three tick replenishment in your direction within three ticks of the absorption printing." `[ABS p.4]`: "Price needs to move in your direction within three ticks of the absorption for it to count as replenished." `[ABS p.5]`: "Plot the median line: if price is sitting above it, the overall directional aggression in Delta is bullish … you only use it in reversal type of scenarios." `[ABS p.6]`: "is this at an extreme, a shelf, a ledge, a low volume node, a minor volume node, or is it in the middle of balance where the signature means nothing." `[ABS p.7]`: "Previous day's VAH and VAL, more usable." `[ABS p.9]`: "enter on the test of the reward system, not on the wall itself." `[ABS p.11]`: "At value area high, a high Delta spike of buyers marks the opposition losing control, sellers absorbing that spike passively, and price rejecting lower." and "At value area low, the mirror image: a high Delta spike of buyers this time regaining control, sellers being absorbed at the bottom, price rejecting back up into balance"; the p.11 figure labels both spikes with the same text ("High delta spike of buyers (Opposition losing control) being abs passively by sellers"), which its own caption acknowledges, so the VAL reading comes from the body text. `[ABS p.4]` figure: the wall is drawn as a horizontal band, the reward-system retest returns into that band, and the CVD median is a straight line on the CVD panel; `[ABS p.9]` figure: the sponge is a thick red band with the effort arrow into it.
- **Procedure.** (1) Location gate: the absorption must sit at a real extreme (shelf ledge, LVN, minor node, prior-day VA edge); prints within `tR` of the prior-day POC are excluded (coin-flip claim). (2) Effort: `flow.absorption.A` at the level (aggressor into a passive wall, advance ≤ 2 ticks). (3) Reward system `flow.reward.3tick`: price moves ≥ 3 ticks in the reversal direction from the absorption price within the next 3 ticks of tape travel — read as: within a window in which price has not moved > 3 ticks against the reversal (the printed phrase "within three ticks of the absorption printing" is a price window, not a time window). (4) Second opposite aggression: aggressive volume on the reversal side ≥ the session's rolling q75 (named) after the reward. (5) Entry object = the retest of the reward-system price (touch `t2` of the level after the reward) — recorded, not traded. (6) CVD-median check (price above/below the session CVD median) — blocked with the tape. (7) Delta-spike row: per-1m |Δ| ≥ q95 (named) at prior-day VAH/VAL vs at POC, recorded as spike sign × extreme × outcome direction — in the lesson both spikes are buy-delta spikes, absorbed at VAH (reject lower) and regaining control at VAL (reject higher), so the sign is never read as the absorbed side; outcome = reversal away from the extreme ≥ 0.25·R within 15 min. The wall and the reward retest in (5) are drawn as a band (p.4, p.9), so the retest tolerance is the band height (3 ticks per `[DOM6 p.4]`, named).
- **Constants.** Printed: 3 ticks; 27%; 72–80%. Source-unspecified: q75, q95, reversal size.
- **Different object if.** The absorption is taken at the POC (excluded), or the reward is measured in time rather than in ticks.
- **Fixture.** VAH 120.00 (prior day). Absorption print at 120.25 (buy aggression absorbed, advance ≤ 2 ticks). Path after: 120.25 → 120.00 → 119.75 → 119.50 (3 ticks down) with max adverse 120.50 (1 tick) → `reward_3tick = 1`. Opposite aggression: 2-min sell volume 2,900 ≥ q75 (2,600) ✓ → `second_aggression = 1`. Failure-share fixture: 100 absorptions, 27 without reward fail → expected 0.27 if the claim holds.
- **Code.** **match** `absorption_a` exists (6–9 H/L only); no reward-3tick, no location gate, no delta spike. CVD median blocked. The 2026-09-10 rescoring wires `family_tape.py` `f08_reward_3tick` = `formulas_flow.py::reward_3tick` after an AM absorption print (3-tick move, no location gate, no second aggression, no delta-spike row); `r_f08_abs_four_check` exists.

### R-F09 — STOP three-step read with the four absorption stages · pass 0.0000 (rescored 2026-09-10, n 647)

- **Source.** `[STOP p.6]`: "location of price, reward vs result, delta as a filter. Three checks, in that order." `[STOP p.7]`: "Buy aggression followed by upticks. Sell aggression followed by downticks that stick." `[STOP p.9]`: "you want the opposite side to come in and thicken, and you want the losing side to thin out. Doubles and triples where your side acts, singles where the losers used to be." `[STOP p.10]`: "01 Initial defense. Aggression hits the level, volume runs above average, price holds … 02 Replenishment. The level refreshes as it keeps getting hit … My minimum filter is three ticks of replenishment … 03 Exhaustion. The aggressor's volume starts declining and delta turns against them … 04 Lift-off. The absorber flips from passive to aggressive and price accelerates away … absorption fails roughly 27% of the time when there's no pacing confirmation behind it." `[STOP p.12]`: "two upticks, which is my minimum, two to four, for calling it a reward system." `[STOP p.14]`: "Timing. My entry sits within a tick or two of that confirmation."
- **Procedure.** Stage 1 = `flow.absorption.A` at the thesis level (2-min aggressive volume ≥ q90, advance ≤ 2 ticks). Stage 2 = replenishment ≥ 3 ticks (BBO reload at the touch = `flow.absorption.B`, blocked; off-touch needs depth). Stage 3 = exhaustion: the aggressor's print sizes thin (`flow.digits.thinning`: median aggressor print size over the last 20 prints falls from ≥ 10 lots to single digits; named) and the 1-minute delta sign turns against the aggressor. Stage 4 = lift-off: 2–4 consecutive upticks (downticks) of the last trade price without an intervening downtick (`flow.reward.3tick` at 2/3/4 ticks; the STOP minimum is 2).
